<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the Hilbert–Smith conjecture in every finite dimension: every locally compact second-countable Hausdorff group acting faithfully and jointly continuously on a connected Hausdorff second-countable finite-dimensional topological manifold without boundary is a Lie group.

- **claim-hilbert-smith** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-lie)): Let $n\geq1$. A locally compact second-countable Hausdorff group acting faithfully and jointly continuously on a connected Hausdorff second-countable topological $n$-manifold without boundary is a Lie group.
  - Explanation: Every faithful, jointly continuous action of a locally compact second-countable Hausdorff group on a connected boundaryless finite-dimensional manifold forces the group to be a Lie group.
- **claim-p-adic-exclusion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $n\geq1$ be an integer and $p$ a prime. Every jointly continuous action of $\mathbb Z_p$ on a connected Hausdorff second-countable topological $n$-manifold without boundary has nonzero kernel. Consequently the action factors through a finite quotient of $\mathbb Z_p$.
  - Explanation: No jointly continuous action of the p-adic integers on a connected boundaryless manifold is faithful, so every such action factors through a finite quotient.
- **claim-simplex-generators** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cat-simplex-generators)): In either definition, closed simplices suffice in place of all compact finite polyhedra. Every object of $\mathcal T(X)$, respectively $\mathcal P(X)$, is a direct summand of an object of $\mathcal T^0(X)$, respectively $\mathcal P^0(X)$. In particular these summand closures are triangulated and thick.
  - Explanation: Closed simplices already generate the test categories, and the summand closures are triangulated and thick.
- **claim-test-duality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-test-duality)): The Verdier operation restricts to a triangulated duality on each of $\mathcal T^0(X)$, $\mathcal T(X)$, $\mathcal P^0(X)$, and $\mathcal P(X)$. In particular, for every object $A$ in one of these categories, $\mathcal D_X A$ belongs to the same category and the canonical map $$\eta_A:A\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal D_X^2A$$ is an isomorphism. There is a compact $K\subset X$ outside which both $A$ and $\mathcal D_X A$ vanish.
  - Explanation: Verdier duality restricts to the four test categories, biduality is invertible on them, and objects and their duals have compact support.
- **claim-test-pushforward** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-test-pushforward)): For an allowed map $g:X\to Y$, direct image induces exact functors $$g_*:\mathcal T^0(X)\longrightarrow\mathcal T^0(Y), \qquad g_*:\mathcal T(X)\longrightarrow\mathcal T(Y),$$ and the analogous functors for $\mathcal P^0,\mathcal P$. They are given by $Rg_!\simeq Rg_*$ on these categories, commute coherently with Verdier duality and its shifts, and are compatible with composition. For an open inclusion $j:U\hookrightarrow X$, this functor is $j_!$ and is fully faithful.
  - Explanation: Direct image along an allowed map preserves the test categories, agrees with proper direct image, commutes with duality, and open extension by zero is fully faithful.
- **claim-symmetric-cone** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cat-symmetric-cone)): Let $(\#,\eta)$ be a triangulated duality with exactness sign $\delta\in\{1,-1\}$ on a category in which multiplication by $2$ is invertible on every morphism group. Let $u:L\to\#L$ be symmetric, without an invertibility assumption. In a triangle $$L\xrightarrow{u}\#L\xrightarrow{v}C\xrightarrow{w}L[1],$$ the cone has a nonsingular symmetric form $\psi:C\xrightarrow{\sim}[1]\#C$ for the translated duality. It is neutral and can be chosen to satisfy $$\label{cat:cone-compatibility} \psi v=-[1](\#w), \qquad [1](\#v)\psi=\delta[1](\eta_L)w,$$ with the dual shift comparisons understood. For $\#=\mathcal D_{X,r+1}$ this is a neutral form of degree $r$.
  - Explanation: The cone of a symmetric morphism carries a canonical nonsingular neutral symmetric form for the translated duality.
- **claim-interval-products** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cat-interval-products)): For $A\in\mathcal T(X)$ and $B\in\mathcal C_I$, the external product $B\boxtimes A$ belongs to $\mathcal T(I\times X)$; the same assertion holds for $\mathcal P$. For all integers $s,r$, the natural map $$\label{cat:external-duality} \mu_{s,r}:\mathcal D_{I,s}B\boxtimes\mathcal D_{X,r}A \xrightarrow{\sim} \mathcal D_{I\times X,s+r}(B\boxtimes A)$$ is an isomorphism. The map is adjoint to the product of evaluations, with the tensor shift signs; it commutes with proper direct image, duality, and the trace maps. In particular the product of symmetric pairings is symmetric for the sum of their degrees.
  - Explanation: External products with interval objects stay in the test categories and the natural product duality comparison is an isomorphism.
- **claim-endpoint-forms** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cat-endpoint-forms)): Let $(A,\alpha)$ be a symmetric degree-$r$ form in $\mathcal T(X)$ or $\mathcal P(X)$, and let $i_t:X\to I\times X$ be $x\mapsto(t,x)$. There is a neutral degree-$r$ form on $$E\boxtimes A\simeq(i_0)_*A\oplus(i_1)_*A$$ whose two diagonal forms are $c(i_0)_*\alpha$ and $-c(i_1)_*\alpha$, for a common nonzero real scalar $c$. There are no off-diagonal terms.
  - Explanation: A neutral form on the sum of the two endpoint objects has diagonal pieces equal to a common scalar times the pushed form and its negative.
- **claim-homotopy-invariance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-homotopy)): If $h:I\times X\to Y$ is a continuous homotopy of maps of test spaces, its endpoint maps induce equal homomorphisms $$h_{0*}=h_{1*}:E_r(X)\longrightarrow E_r(Y) \qquad(r\in\mathbb Z).$$ The same assertion holds for $W_r(\mathcal P(-))$ under the allowed semialgebraic homotopies. In particular, homotopy equivalences induce isomorphisms, and a nonempty contractible test space has the Witt groups of a point. The semialgebraic assertion uses a contraction in the allowed semialgebraic category.
  - Explanation: Homotopic maps of test spaces induce equal maps on Witt groups, in the continuous and the semialgebraic settings.
- **claim-cutoff** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cutoff)): Let $K\subset O\subset X$, where $K$ is closed in $X$ and $O$ is open. For every $A\in\mathcal B^0(X)$ there is a morphism $$s:A'\longrightarrow A$$ such that $A'$ is extended from $\mathcal B^0(O)$, its cohomology vanishes outside a compact subset of $O$, and $s$ restricts to an isomorphism on some open neighborhood of $K$.
  - Explanation: Any generated object can be replaced by one supported compactly inside a prescribed open set without changing its germ near the closed subset.
- **claim-compact-support** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-compact-support)): For an open inclusion $j:U\hookrightarrow X$, the essential image of $j_!: \mathcal B(U)\to\mathcal B(X)$ consists exactly of those objects whose cohomology vanishes outside some compact subset of $U$. This image is a thick, duality-stable subcategory.
  - Explanation: Objects coming from an open set are exactly those with cohomology vanishing outside a compact subset of it, and this subcategory is thick and duality stable.
- **claim-germ-description** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-germs)): Restriction induces natural isomorphisms $$\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathcal B(X)/\mathcal B(U)}(A,B) \simeq \operatorname*{colim}_{N\supset X\setminus U} \mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathsf D(N)}(A\|_N,B\|_N)$$ for every $A,B\in\mathcal B(X)$. These isomorphisms respect composition, open restriction of duality, and restriction of distinguished triangles. The quotient here is the Verdier quotient itself.
  - Explanation: Morphisms in the Verdier quotient are germs of derived morphisms near the closed complement.
- **claim-witt-localization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-witt-localization)): For every open $U\subset X$ and every $r\in\mathbb Z$, there is a natural long exact sequence $$\label{loc:eq-witt-localization} \cdots\longrightarrow W_r(\mathcal B(U)) \longrightarrow W_r(\mathcal B(X)) \longrightarrow W_r(\mathcal B(X)/\mathcal B(U)) \xrightarrow{\partial}W_{r-1}(\mathcal B(U)) \longrightarrow\cdots.$$ The sequence is natural for $\mathcal P\to\mathcal T$ and for the pushforwards of [prop:test-pushforward](#prop:test-pushforward): a map $g:X\to X'$ with $g(U)\subset U'$ induces a morphism from the sequence for $(X,U)$ to the sequence for $(X',U')$.
  - Explanation: There is a natural long exact localization sequence of Witt groups for an open subset.
- **claim-open-cover-dense** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-open-cover)): If $X=U\cup V$ with $U,V$ open, extension by zero induces a fully faithful exact functor preserving duality $$\label{loc:eq-open-cover} \mathcal B(V)/\mathcal B(U\cap V) \longrightarrow\mathcal B(X)/\mathcal B(U).$$ Its replete image is dense: every object of the target is an actual summand of an object in the image. These functors commute with $\mathcal P\to\mathcal T$.
  - Explanation: For an open cover by two sets, extension by zero between the quotients is fully faithful with dense image.
- **claim-dense-doubling** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-dense-doubling)): Let $\mathcal A\subset\mathcal C$ be a full replete triangulated subcategory stable under duality, and suppose it is dense: each object of $\mathcal C$ is a summand of an object of $\mathcal A$. Assume these categories admit triangular Witt groups. In every degree, the inclusion homomorphism $$\iota:W_r(\mathcal A)\longrightarrow W_r(\mathcal C)$$ has kernel and cokernel annihilated by $2$. More precisely, there is a homomorphism $d:W_r(\mathcal C)\to W_r(\mathcal A)$ satisfying $$\iota d=2\mathrm{id}, \qquad d\iota=2\mathrm{id}.$$ No closure of $\mathcal A$ under arbitrary summands is required.
  - Explanation: For a dense duality-stable triangulated subcategory, a doubling homomorphism shows the inclusion on Witt groups has kernel and cokernel killed by the stated integer.
- **claim-open-cover-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-cor-open-cover-bound)): The map on Witt groups induced by <a href="#loc:eq-open-cover" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="loc:eq-open-cover">[loc:eq-open-cover]</a> has kernel and cokernel annihilated by $2$, in every degree and for either test category.
  - Explanation: Combining density of the open-cover inclusion with the doubling theorem bounds the kernel and cokernel by the same small integer in every degree.
- **claim-point-groups** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lat-point-groups)): For every integer $r$, the comparison at a point is an isomorphism, and $$F_r(\mathrm{pt})=E_r(\mathrm{pt})= \begin{cases} \mathbb Z,&r\equiv0\pmod4,\\ 0,&r\not\equiv0\pmod4. \end{cases}$$ In degree zero the indicated isomorphism is signature. On $S^0$ both theories are the direct sum of two copies of the point theory, and the map to the point is addition.
  - Explanation: At a point both Witt theories agree, equal the integers in degrees divisible by four and vanish otherwise.
- **claim-finite-chases** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lat-finite-chases)): In a commutative diagram of exact rows $$\begin{array}{ccccccccc} A_1&\longrightarrow&A_2&\longrightarrow&A_3&\longrightarrow&A_4&\longrightarrow&A_5\\ {\scriptstyle f_1}\downarrow&&{\scriptstyle f_2}\downarrow&& {\scriptstyle f_3}\downarrow&&{\scriptstyle f_4}\downarrow&& {\scriptstyle f_5}\downarrow\\ B_1&\longrightarrow&B_2&\longrightarrow&B_3&\longrightarrow&B_4&\longrightarrow&B_5, \end{array}$$ suppose the kernels and cokernels of $f_1,f_2,f_4,f_5$ are annihilated by $q$. Then the kernel and cokernel of $f_3$ are annihilated by $q^3$.* *Suppose also that a commutative square is written as $$\begin{array}{ccc} A&\xrightarrow{i}&A'\\ {\scriptstyle f}\downarrow&&\downarrow{\scriptstyle g}\\ B&\xrightarrow{j}&B'. \end{array}$$ If both horizontal kernels and cokernels are annihilated by $2$, and the kernel and cokernel of $f$ are annihilated by $q$, then those of $g$ are annihilated by $4q$.
  - Explanation: Two diagram chases propagate annihilation of kernels and cokernels from outer maps or horizontal maps to the middle or right map with controlled exponents.
- **claim-bounded-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-bounded-comparison)): For every $j\ge0$ there is a nonnegative integer $e_j$, independent of $r$, such that the kernel and cokernel of $$F_r(S^j)\longrightarrow E_r(S^j)$$ are annihilated by $2^{e_j}$ for every integer $r$.
  - Explanation: The comparison between semialgebraic and continuous Witt groups of a sphere has kernel and cokernel killed by a power of two depending only on the sphere dimension.
- **claim-sphere-rank** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-sphere-rank)): For either $G_r=F_r$ or $G_r=E_r$, and for $j\ge0$, $$G_r(S^j)\otimes\mathbb Q\simeq \bigl(G_r(\mathrm{pt})\otimes\mathbb Q\bigr) \oplus\bigl(G_{r-j}(\mathrm{pt})\otimes\mathbb Q\bigr).$$ In particular, for the fixed odd integer $d$, both $F_d(S^d)\otimes\mathbb Q$ and $E_d(S^d)\otimes\mathbb Q$ are one-dimensional rational vector spaces.
  - Explanation: Rationally, the Witt groups of a sphere split into two point contributions, so the diagonal odd case is a line.
- **claim-generic-signature** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-generic-signature)): Every finite collection of objects of $\mathcal P(S^d)$ has finite dimensional locally constant cohomology on a common dense open subset of $S^d$. On a sufficiently small oriented ball there, a degree-$d$ form has an integral signature on its degree-zero cohomology. For any finite Witt relation one can choose such a ball simultaneously for all objects and lagrangian diagrams in the relation; the signatures then respect that relation.* *Consequently $u_{F,d}$ is nontorsion and $$\begin{gathered} F_d(S^d)\otimes\mathbb Q=\mathbb Q\overline{u}_{F,d},\\ \mathop{\mathrm{im}}\bigl(F_d(S^d)\to F_d(S^d)\otimes\mathbb Q\bigr)=\mathbb Z\overline{u}_{F,d}. \end{gathered}$$
  - Explanation: Finite semialgebraic data have locally constant finite cohomology on a dense open set, giving an integral signature that shows the orientation class is nontorsion with integral image.
- **claim-sphere-lattice** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-sphere-lattice)): Let $d\geq3$ be odd, with orientation class $u_d$ as above. There is a positive power of $2$, denoted by $L_d$, such that $$\label{lat:lattice} \begin{gathered} E_d(S^d)\otimes\mathbb Q=\mathbb Q\overline{u}_d,\\ \mathop{\mathrm{im}}\bigl(E_d(S^d)\to E_d(S^d)\otimes\mathbb Q\bigr) \subseteq L_d^{-1}\mathbb Z\overline{u}_d. \end{gathered}$$ One may take $L_d=2^{e_d}$. This denominator is fixed by the sphere dimension and comparison. It is independent of every later action, test map, character partition, prime $p$, and exponent $k$.
  - Explanation: The rational image of the continuous sphere Witt group lies in a lattice with a fixed power-of-two denominator independent of any later choices.
- **claim-degree-action** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-degree-action)): Let $d\geq3$ be odd. If $f:S^d\to S^d$ is continuous and has degree $s\in\mathbb Z$, then $$f_*\overline{u}_d=s\overline{u}_d\quad\text{in }E_d(S^d)\otimes\mathbb Q.$$ Consequently $f_*$ acts on this entire rational group by multiplication by $s$. More generally, if $N$ is a connected closed oriented triangulable $d$-manifold and $f:N\to S^d$ has degree $s$, its pushed orientation form has rational class $s\overline{u}_d$.
  - Explanation: A map of the odd sphere acts on the rational orientation class by multiplication by its degree.
- **claim-contractible-quotient** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-contractible-quotient)): Let $d\geq3$ be odd. Let $X$ be any test space for $\mathcal T$, and let $U\subset X$ be a nonempty contractible open subset. Then the quotient functor induces an integral isomorphism $$\label{lat:contractible-isomorphism} E_d(X)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ } W_d\bigl(\mathcal T(X)/\mathcal T(U)\bigr).$$ In particular this applies to compact test polyhedra and to their manifold-with-boundary models. It is natural for continuous maps $g:X\to X'$ with $g(U)\subset U'$, whenever $U'$ is also a nonempty contractible open subset.
  - Explanation: Quotienting by a contractible open set does not change the integral Witt group in the relevant odd degree.
- **claim-chart-reduction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-chart-reduction)): There are an open subgroup $G\cong\mathbb Z_p$ and a nonempty connected $G$-invariant open subset $O$ of a coordinate chart of $M$ such that $G$ acts faithfully and preserves orientation on $O$. In particular, we may regard $O$ as the actual open subset of $\mathbb R^n$ supplied by that chart.
  - Explanation: A faithful action of the p-adic integers yields a faithful orientation-preserving action of an open subgroup on a connected invariant chart subset.
- **claim-collapse-compactification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-collapse)): The map $$c:S^d\longrightarrow W^+, \qquad c(z)= \begin{cases} z,&z\in W,\\ \infty,&z\notin W, \end{cases}$$ is continuous. The action on $W$ extends to a jointly continuous action on $W^+$ fixing $\infty$. Both $W^+$ and its orbit space by any closed subgroup of $G$ are compact metrizable spaces.
  - Explanation: The collapse map is continuous, the action extends to the one-point compactification, and the compactification and its orbit spaces are compact metrizable.
- **claim-averaged-test** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-averaged-test)): After replacing $G$ by an open subgroup and identifying that subgroup with $\mathbb Z_p$, set $$q:W^+\longrightarrow Y=W^+/G, \qquad y_\infty=q(\infty).$$ There is a continuous map $a:Y\to S^d$ such that $$\deg(aqc)=1. \label{top:degree-one}$$ Moreover, $aq$ is homotopic on $W^+$ to a degree-one pinch supported in a round ball compactly contained in $W$.
  - Explanation: Haar averaging over a small open subgroup produces an invariant degree-one test map on the orbit space.
- **claim-orbit-injectivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-orbit-injectivity)): Let $E$ be a compact metrizable space with a continuous $G$-action, and let $r:E\to E/G$ be its orbit map. For any field $k$, in the bounded-below derived category of sheaves one has $$Rr_*\underline{k}_{E}\simeq r_*\underline{k}_{E}.$$ For $k=\mathbb Q$ the unit $\underline{\mathbb Q}_{E/G}\to r_*\underline{\mathbb Q}_{E}$ has a natural sheaf retraction. Consequently $$r^*:\check H^j(E/G;\mathbb Q)\longrightarrow\check H^j(E;\mathbb Q)$$ is injective for every $j$.
  - Explanation: Derived and ordinary direct images agree for orbit maps, and rational Cech cohomology pulls back injectively from the orbit space.
- **claim-odd-sphere-null** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-odd-sphere-null)): Let $d\geq3$ be odd. If $K$ is compact metrizable and $v:K\to S^d$ satisfies $v^*\gamma_d=0$, then there is a positive integer $s$ and a degree $s$ map $D_s:S^d\to S^d$ for which $D_sv$ is nullhomotopic.
  - Explanation: A rationally trivial map into an odd sphere becomes nullhomotopic after composing with a suitable positive-degree self-map.
- **claim-finite-sheets** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-sheets)): For every integer $k\ge1$, put $m=p^k$ and $\zeta=\exp(2\pi i/m)$. There exists a nonempty open $V\subset Y\setminus\{y_\infty\}$ such that the finite quotient map $$W/(p^kG)\longrightarrow W/G=Y\setminus\{y_\infty\}$$ over $V$ is a disjoint union of $m$ homeomorphisms onto $V$. There is a locally constant function $$\beta:q^{-1}(V)\longrightarrow\{1,\zeta,\ldots,\zeta^{m-1}\} \subset S^1$$ satisfying $$\beta(gw)=\zeta^{\bar g}\beta(w), \qquad \bar g\in G/p^kG=\mathbb Z/m. \label{top:sheet-multiplier}$$
  - Explanation: Faithfulness gives an open region of the orbit space over which the finite quotient is a disjoint union of sheets, with a locally constant character multiplier.
- **claim-neighborhood-extension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-top-neighborhood-extension)): Let $Y_0$ be compact metrizable, $F_0\subset Y_0$ closed, and $f:Y_0\to S^d$ continuous. Every homotopy $H:F_0\times[0,1]\to S^d$ starting at $f\|_{F_0}$ is the restriction of a homotopy on $Y_0$ starting at $f$.
  - Explanation: A homotopy into the sphere given on a closed subset of a compact metrizable space extends to the whole space.
- **claim-move-test** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-move-test)): For every nonempty open $V\subset Y\setminus\{y_\infty\}$, set $F=Y\setminus V$. There are a positive integer $s$, a degree $s$ map $D_s:S^d\to S^d$, and a map $h:Y\to S^d$ such that $$h\simeq D_sa, \qquad h\|_F\equiv b$$ for some $b\in S^d$. In particular $h(y_\infty)=b$ and $q^{-1}h^{-1}(S^d\setminus\{b\})\subset q^{-1}(V)\subset W$.
  - Explanation: A positive-degree postcomposition of the test map can be deformed to a map constant outside a prescribed nonempty open set of the orbit space.
- **claim-character-coproduct** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-character-coproduct)): There are canonical isomorphisms $$\label{char:orbit-sum} Rq_*\underline{\mathbb C}_{W^+}\simeq\mathcal H \simeq\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\mathcal H_\lambda.$$ For every continuous map $t:Y\to X$ to a compact Hausdorff space and every $D\subset\Lambda$, the natural map $$\label{char:derived-sum} \bigoplus_{\lambda\in D}Rt_*\mathcal H_\lambda \longrightarrow Rt_*\mathcal H_D$$ is an isomorphism in the bounded-below derived category of real sheaves on $X$. The sum on the left is a coproduct in the ambient derived category.
  - Explanation: The orbit sheaf splits into character eigensheaves and direct images of the eigensheaf sums are coproducts in the derived category.
- **claim-orthogonality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-orthogonality)): If $D,E\subset\Lambda$ are disjoint, the cross pairing between $B_D$ and $B_E$ induced by $\alpha_t\|_{X_t^\circ}$ is zero. In particular, the projectors associated with any finite partition of $\Lambda$ are self-adjoint.
  - Explanation: Eigensheaf parts for disjoint character sets are orthogonal for the pushed pairing, so character projectors are self-adjoint.
- **claim-sum-classes** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-sum-classes)): Let $t:Y\to X$ be a compact test map as in <a href="#char:test-form" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="char:test-form">[char:test-form]</a>, with a contractible open neighborhood basis at $b_t$. For every finite partition $\Lambda=D_0\sqcup\cdots\sqcup D_{m-1}$, its character classes satisfy the integral identity $$\label{char:class-sum} \sum_{i=0}^{m-1}z_i(t)=2[A_t,\alpha_t]\quad\text{in }E_d(X).$$
  - Explanation: The integral character classes of any finite partition add up to twice the class of the pushed orientation form.
- **claim-class-covariance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-class-covariance)): Let $t:Y\to X$ be a compact test map as in <a href="#char:test-form" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="char:test-form">[char:test-form]</a>, and let $g:X\to X'$ be a continuous map of compact tests. Suppose $X$ has a contractible neighborhood basis at $b_t$ and $X'$ has one at $b_{gt}=g(b_t)$. For every fixed finite partition of $\Lambda$, $$\label{char:covariance} g_*z_i(t)=z_i(gt)\quad\text{in }E_d(X').$$
  - Explanation: Character classes are natural under continuous maps of compact tests with contractible neighborhood bases at the distinguished point.
- **claim-class-homotopy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-class-homotopy)): For a fixed partition of $\Lambda$, homotopic maps $t_0,t_1:Y\to S^d$ satisfy $z_i(t_0)=z_i(t_1)$ in $E_d(S^d)$.
  - Explanation: Character classes of homotopic maps into the sphere coincide.
- **claim-equal-parts** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-equal-parts)): Let $k\geq1$, and let $V$ and its character multiplier $\beta$ be as in [lem:finite-sheets](#lem:finite-sheets). If $h:Y\to S^d$ is constant on $Y\setminus V$, then the classes for <a href="#char:cyclic-partition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="char:cyclic-partition">[char:cyclic-partition]</a> satisfy $$z_0(h)=z_1(h)=\cdots=z_{m-1}(h)\quad\text{in }E_d(S^d).$$
  - Explanation: For a test concentrated on the finite sheet region, a cyclic isometry shows all character classes are equal.
- **claim-contradiction-chart** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-contradiction)): For every $n\geq1$ and every prime $p$, an orientation-preserving continuous $\mathbb Z_p$-action on a connected open subset of $\mathbb R^n$ has nonzero kernel.
  - Explanation: A faithful orientation-preserving action of the p-adic integers on a connected open subset of Euclidean space would split the orientation class into too many equal integral parts, so the kernel is nonzero.
- **claim-product-shift-sign** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cat-product-shift-sign)): Let $B\in\mathcal C_I$, $A\in\mathcal T(X)$ (or the corresponding semialgebraic category), and $s,r\in\mathbb Z$. Write $$\gamma_s(B)=[-s]\kappa_1: \mathcal D_{I,s}(B[1])\longrightarrow(\mathcal D_{I,s}B)[-1].$$ Use the first-factor shift comparisons $$\begin{aligned} \sigma: B[1]\boxtimes A&\xrightarrow{\sim}(B\boxtimes A)[1],\\ \epsilon: (\mathcal D_{I,s}B)[-1]\boxtimes\mathcal D_{X,r}A &\xrightarrow{\sim}(\mathcal D_{I,s}B\boxtimes\mathcal D_{X,r}A)[-1]. \end{aligned}$$ They introduce no sign under <a href="#cat:tensor-output-shifts" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cat:tensor-output-shifts">[cat:tensor-output-shifts]</a>. Indicate the inputs of the product comparison by superscripts and put $$\widetilde\mu_{s,r} =(\mathcal D_{I\times X,s+r}\sigma)^{-1}\mu_{s,r}^{B[1],A}, \qquad \lambda=\epsilon\circ(\gamma_s(B)\boxtimes1).$$ Then the two routes to the suspended total dual satisfy $$\label{cat:suspension-comparison} \gamma_{s+r}(B\boxtimes A)\,\widetilde\mu_{s,r} =(-1)^r\mu_{s,r}^{B,A}[-1]\,\lambda.$$ The scalar $(-1)^r$ is independent of $A$, its cohomological degrees, and the point of $I$.
  - Explanation: Suspending the first product input produces a sign discrepancy that depends only on the parity of the second shift parameter.

        "exact_quote": "Let $(\\#,\\eta)$ be a triangulated duality with exactness sign $\\delta\\in\\{1,-1\\}$ on a category in which multiplication by $2$ is invertible on every morphism group. Let $u:L\\to\\#L$ be symmetric, without an invertibility assumption. In a triangle $$L\\xrightarrow{u}\\#L\\xrightarrow{v}C\\xrightarrow{w}L[1],$$ the cone has a nonsingular symmetric form $\\psi:C\\xrightarrow{\\sim}[1]\\#C$ for the translated duality. It is neutral and can be chosen to satisfy $$\\label{cat:cone-compatibility}\n \\psi v=-[1](\\#w),\n \\qquad [1](\\#v)\\psi=\\delta[1](\\eta_L)w,$$ with the dual shift comparisons understood. For $\\#=\\mathcal D_{X,r+1}$ this is a neutral form of degree $r$.",
        "source_id": "PM-304.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cat-interval-products",
        "exact_quote": "For $A\\in\\mathcal T(X)$ and $B\\in\\mathcal C_I$, the external product $B\\boxtimes A$ belongs to $\\mathcal T(I\\times X)$; the same assertion holds for $\\mathcal P$. For all integers $s,r$, the natural map $$\\label{cat:external-duality}\n \\mu_{s,r}:\\mathcal D_{I,s}B\\boxtimes\\mathcal D_{X,r}A\n \\xrightarrow{\\sim}\n \\mathcal D_{I\\times X,s+r}(B\\boxtimes A)$$ is an isomorphism. The map is adjoint to the product of evaluations, with the tensor shift signs; it commutes with proper direct image, duality, and the trace maps. In particular the product of symmetric pairings is symmetric for the sum of their degrees.",
        "source_id": "PM-304.1"
      },

The map is adjoint to the product of evaluations, with the tensor shift signs; it commutes with proper direct image, duality, and the trace maps.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-homotopy",
        "exact_quote": "If $h:I\\times X\\to Y$ is a continuous homotopy of maps of test spaces, its endpoint maps induce equal homomorphisms $$h_{0*}=h_{1*}:E_r(X)\\longrightarrow E_r(Y)\n \\qquad(r\\in\\mathbb Z).$$ The same assertion holds for $W_r(\\mathcal P(-))$ under the allowed semialgebraic homotopies. In particular, homotopy equivalences induce isomorphisms, and a nonempty contractible test space has the Witt groups of a point. The semialgebraic assertion uses a contraction in the allowed semialgebraic category.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-thm-witt-localization",
        "exact_quote": "For every open $U\\subset X$ and every $r\\in\\mathbb Z$, there is a natural long exact sequence $$\\label{loc:eq-witt-localization}\n \\cdots\\longrightarrow W_r(\\mathcal B(U))\n \\longrightarrow W_r(\\mathcal B(X))\n \\longrightarrow W_r(\\mathcal B(X)/\\mathcal B(U))\n \\xrightarrow{\\partial}W_{r-1}(\\mathcal B(U))\n \\longrightarrow\\cdots.$$ The sequence is natural for $\\mathcal P\\to\\mathcal T$ and for the pushforwards of [prop:test-pushforward](#prop:test-pushforward): a map $g:X\\to X'$ with $g(U)\\subset U'$ induces a morphism from the sequence for $(X,U)$ to the sequence for $(X',U')$.",
        "source_id": "PM-304.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-thm-witt-localization",
          "exact_quote": "For every open $U\\subset X$ and every $r\\in\\mathbb Z$, there is a natural long exact sequence $$\\label{loc:eq-witt-localization}\n \\cdots\\longrightarrow W_r(\\mathcal B(U))\n \\longrightarrow W_r(\\mathcal B(X))\n \\longrightarrow W_r(\\mathcal B(X)/\\mathcal B(U))\n \\xrightarrow{\\partial}W_{r-1}(\\mathcal B(U))\n \\longrightarrow\\cdots.$$ The sequence is natural for $\\mathcal P\\to\\mathcal T$ and for the pushforwards of [prop:test-pushforward](#prop:test-pushforward): a map $g:X\\to X'$ with $g(U)\\subset U'$ induces a morphism from the sequence for $(X,U)$ to the sequence for $(X',U')$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-dense-doubling",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal A\\subset\\mathcal C$ be a full replete triangulated subcategory stable under duality, and suppose it is dense: each object of $\\mathcal C$ is a summand of an object of $\\mathcal A$. Assume these categories admit triangular Witt groups. In every degree, the inclusion homomorphism $$\\iota:W_r(\\mathcal A)\\longrightarrow W_r(\\mathcal C)$$ has kernel and cokernel annihilated by $2$. More precisely, there is a homomorphism $d:W_r(\\mathcal C)\\to W_r(\\mathcal A)$ satisfying $$\\iota d=2\\mathrm{id},\n \\qquad d\\iota=2\\mathrm{id}.$$ No closure of $\\mathcal A$ under arbitrary summands is required.",
        "source_id": "PM-304.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-dense-doubling",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal A\\subset\\mathcal C$ be a full replete triangulated subcategory stable under duality, and suppose it is dense: each object of $\\mathcal C$ is a summand of an object of $\\mathcal A$.           "source_id": "PM-304.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $\\mathcal A\\subset\\mathcal C$ be a full replete triangulated subcategory stable under duality, and suppose it is dense: each object of $\\mathcal C$ is a summand of an object of $\\mathcal A$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-generic-signature",
        "exact_quote": "Every finite collection of objects of $\\mathcal P(S^d)$ has finite dimensional locally constant cohomology on a common dense open subset of $S^d$. On a sufficiently small oriented ball there, a degree-$d$ form has an integral signature on its degree-zero cohomology.         "source_id": "PM-304.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-degree-action",
        "exact_quote": "Let $d\\geq3$ be odd.         "exact_quote": "Let $E$ be a compact metrizable space with a continuous $G$-action, and let $r:E\\to E/G$ be its orbit map.       },

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Test categories** ([source](#stmt-def-test-categories)): Let $\mathcal T^0(X)$ be the smallest full replete triangulated subcategory of $\mathsf D(X)$ containing $$\label{cat:generators} Rf_*\underline{\mathbb R}_{B}, \qquad f:B\longrightarrow X, \qquad B\text{ a compact finite polyhedron},$$ where $f$ is any continuous map. Let $\mathcal T(X)$ be its closure under direct summands in $\mathsf D(X)$.
- **Test space** ([source](#pm-h-8)): A *test space* is a locally compact, finite-dimensional, separable metrizable space of the following kinds: a finite polyhedron, a space homeomorphic to one, an open subset of such a space, or a finite product of these spaces.
- **Faithful action** ([source](#sec:introduction)): An action is *faithful* when its kernel is trivial: no nonidentity element fixes every point.
- **Almost periodic homeomorphism** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a manifold $M$ as in [cor:lie](#cor:lie), a homeomorphism $f$ is *almost periodic* if the cyclic subgroup $\{f^j:j\in\mathbb Z\}$ has compact closure $K$ in $\mathop{\mathrm{Homeo}}(M)$.
- **Triangular lagrangian** ([source](#pm-h-10)): A triangular lagrangian for $(A,\alpha)$ is an arrow $i:L\to A$ completed to a triangle $L\to A\to\#L\to L[1]$ in which the second map is the composite $A\xrightarrow{\alpha}\#A\xrightarrow{\#i}\#L$, with the translated-duality signs, and the triangle is compatible with its dual under the form and biduality, including the connecting morphism.
- **Character group Lambda** ([source](#pm-h-27)): Let $\Lambda=\bigcup_{j\geq 0}\mu_{p^j}\subset S^1$ be the countable group of complex roots of unity of $p$-power order.
- **Character classes** ([source](#stmt-def-character-classes)): For $0\leq i<m$ define $z_i(t)\in E_d(X)$ uniquely by

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-lattice-denominator | fixed sphere lattice denominator | = | $2^{e_d}$ | odd d at least three; independent of action, test map, partition, prime and exponent |
| q-exponent-recursion | comparison exponent e_j | = | $3(9^j-1)/4$ | j at least zero with e_0=0 |
| q-dense-inclusion-annihilator | annihilator of kernel and cokernel for dense inclusions | = | $2$ | every degree |
| q-five-term-chase | annihilator of kernel and cokernel of the middle map | = | $q^3$ | outer maps annihilated by q |
| q-square-chase | annihilator of kernel and cokernel of the right vertical map | = | $4q$ | horizontal maps annihilated by two, left vertical by q |
| q-equal-image-size | common rational image of the character classes | = | $(4/p^k)\overline{u}_d$ | prime p and exponent k at least one |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-simplex** [claim](#stmt-cat-simplex-generators) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cat-simplex-generators): Filter a triangulation by skeleta and use open-complement triangles; show summand classes are triangulated. -> simplex generators and thick summand closures
- **step-duality** [claim](#stmt-prop-test-duality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-test-duality): Compute duality on a simplex, transport through proper images, extend by triangles and summands. -> duality on test categories
- **step-pushforward** [claim](#stmt-prop-test-pushforward) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-test-pushforward): Compact support identifies proper and ordinary direct image; compose generators; check duality compatibility. -> exact pushforward functors
- **step-symmetric-cone** [claim](#stmt-cat-symmetric-cone) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cat-symmetric-cone): Complete a morphism of triangles and symmetrize using invertibility of two. -> neutral symmetric form on the cone
- **step-interval-products** [claim](#stmt-cat-interval-products) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cat-interval-products): Check membership on generators and invertibility of the natural product comparison on interval objects. -> product duality isomorphism
- **step-endpoint-forms** [claim](#stmt-cat-endpoint-forms) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cat-endpoint-forms): Apply the symmetric cone to the interval triangle times the form; disjoint supports force diagonal form. -> neutral form with opposite endpoint pieces
- **step-product-shift-sign** [claim](#stmt-cat-product-shift-sign) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cat-product-shift-sign): Compare Hom shift signs and product evaluation signs along two routes. -> degree-independent sign discrepancy
- **step-homotopy** [claim](#stmt-thm-homotopy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-homotopy): Neutral cylinder form gives equal endpoint classes; push along the homotopy. -> homotopy invariance of Witt groups
- **step-cutoff** [claim](#stmt-lem-cutoff) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cutoff): Cut off simplex generators by a linear function on a subdivision; extend to cones by adjunction. -> compactly supported replacement
- **step-compact-support** [claim](#stmt-prop-compact-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-compact-support): Use cutoff on a splitting of a summand and open-extension adjunction. -> support characterization of open objects
- **step-germs** [claim](#stmt-thm-germs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-germs): Prove fullness and faithfulness of restriction to germs using cutoff and roofs. -> germ description of quotient morphisms
- **step-witt-localization** [claim](#stmt-thm-witt-localization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-witt-localization): Verify hypotheses of the Balmer-Walter localization theorem. -> long exact Witt sequence
- **step-open-cover** [claim](#stmt-prop-open-cover) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-open-cover): Compare germs on cofinal neighborhoods and use cutoff for density. -> dense fully faithful inclusion
- **step-dense-doubling** [claim](#stmt-thm-dense-doubling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-dense-doubling): Define the doubling of a form with a hyperbolic summand and show it descends to Witt groups. -> two-sided doubling homomorphism
- **step-open-cover-bound** [claim](#stmt-loc-cor-open-cover-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-loc-cor-open-cover-bound): Apply the doubling theorem to the image of the open-cover inclusion. -> exponent-two bound
- **step-point-groups** [claim](#stmt-lat-point-groups) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lat-point-groups): Split complexes into cohomology and reduce to symmetric forms over the reals. -> point Witt groups
- **step-chases** [claim](#stmt-lat-finite-chases) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lat-finite-chases): Element-wise diagram chase. -> cubic and fourfold exponent bounds
- **step-bounded-comparison** [claim](#stmt-thm-bounded-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-bounded-comparison): Induct on sphere dimension with the pole cover, localization rows and chases. -> uniform power-of-two bound
- **step-sphere-rank** [claim](#stmt-prop-sphere-rank) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-sphere-rank): Identify reduced groups of suspensions rationally via localization and density. -> rational sphere splitting
- **step-generic-signature** [claim](#stmt-prop-generic-signature) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-generic-signature): Use semialgebraic triviality to get a signature on a small ball respecting finite Witt relations. -> nontorsion orientation class with integral image
- **step-sphere-lattice** [claim](#stmt-thm-sphere-lattice) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-sphere-lattice): Combine bounded comparison with the integral image of the semialgebraic class. -> fixed lattice
- **step-degree-action** [claim](#stmt-prop-degree-action) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-degree-action): Simplicial approximation and local sheet count give the degree. -> action of degree
- **step-contractible-quotient** [claim](#stmt-prop-contractible-quotient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-contractible-quotient): Localization sequence with split injectivity from contractibility. -> integral quotient isomorphism
- **step-chart-reduction** [claim](#stmt-prop-chart-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-chart-reduction): Newman rigidity gives a point with no locally fixing open subgroup; build an invariant chart subset. -> faithful oriented action on a chart subset
- **step-collapse** [claim](#stmt-lem-collapse) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-collapse): Check continuity at infinity and build invariant metrics. -> compactified action
- **step-averaged-test** [claim](#stmt-lem-averaged-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-averaged-test): Average a pinch map over a small open subgroup and normalize. -> degree-one test map
- **step-orbit-injectivity** [claim](#stmt-lem-orbit-injectivity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-orbit-injectivity): Proper base change over zero-dimensional orbits plus rational averaging retraction. -> injective rational pullback
- **step-odd-sphere-null** [claim](#stmt-lem-odd-sphere-null) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-odd-sphere-null): Nerve approximation and an odd-sphere telescope of Eilenberg-MacLane type using Serre finiteness. -> nullhomotopy after degree map
- **step-finite-sheets** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-sheets) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-sheets): Use faithfulness to find a point whose stabilizer lies in the subgroup, then separate translates. -> open region with labeled sheets
- **step-extension** [claim](#stmt-top-neighborhood-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-top-neighborhood-extension): Tietze extension then radial normalization. -> extended homotopy
- **step-move-test** [claim](#stmt-prop-move-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-move-test): Move the pinch into the region, apply orbit injectivity, odd-sphere nullhomotopy, then extend. -> concentrated test map
- **step-coproduct** [claim](#stmt-lem-character-coproduct) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-character-coproduct): Eigen-decomposition of finite cyclic representations; soft resolutions on compact fibers. -> character coproduct isomorphisms
- **step-orthogonality** [claim](#stmt-lem-orthogonality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-orthogonality): Adjointness of the generator and scalar operators and an invertible operator on the target part. -> orthogonal character parts
- **step-sum-classes** [claim](#stmt-prop-sum-classes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-sum-classes): Explicit matrix isometry built from permutations and projectors. -> sum identity for classes
- **step-covariance** [claim](#stmt-prop-class-covariance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-class-covariance): Pushforward of quotient roofs with germ comparison near the complement. -> naturality of classes
- **step-class-homotopy** [claim](#stmt-prop-class-homotopy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-class-homotopy): Factor close stages through the close-pair test space and use homotopic projections. -> homotopy invariance of classes
- **step-equal-parts** [claim](#stmt-prop-equal-parts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-equal-parts): Multiplier isometry cyclically permutes character parts. -> equal classes
- **step-contradiction** [claim](#stmt-thm-contradiction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-contradiction): Compare equal rational images with the fixed lattice for large exponent. -> nonzero kernel
- **step-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-contradiction-2): Chart reduction contradicts the chart theorem; nonzero closed subgroups of the p-adic integers are open. -> p-adic exclusion
- **step-lie** [claim](#stmt-cor-lie) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-contradiction-3): Classical reduction to embedded p-adic integers contradicts the exclusion theorem. -> Lie group conclusion

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-padic-exclusion**: For a prime p and dimension n at least one, any jointly continuous action of the p-adic integers on a connected boundaryless second-countable Hausdorff n-manifold has a nonzero kernel, so it factors through a finite quotient.
- **inv-lie-conclusion**: A locally compact second-countable Hausdorff group acting faithfully and jointly continuously on a connected boundaryless finite-dimensional manifold is a Lie group.
- **inv-fixed-sphere-lattice**: For odd d at least three, the rational image of the integral Witt group of the sphere lies in a fixed lattice with a power-of-two denominator that does not depend on any action, test map, prime or exponent.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-boundary-manifolds**: Apply the Lie-group conclusion to a group acting on a manifold that has a boundary. -> The chart reduction and the whole argument are proved only for manifolds without boundary; nothing is asserted otherwise.
- **anti-nonfaithful-padic**: Expect a non-Lie group such as the p-adic integers to be excluded even when the action is not faithful. -> The theorem only forces a nonzero kernel and a finite quotient, so non-faithful actions are not ruled out.
- **anti-explicit-exponent**: Treat the exponent bookkeeping as a sharp, optimized bound to be computed and used as a numerical certificate. -> The bound is explicitly not optimized and only its existence enters the proof, so it is no certified minimum.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main results are existence and obstruction theorems proved by contradiction through derived sheaf categories, Witt groups and homotopy theory of odd spheres; no finite procedure with checkable inputs and outputs is stated. The only explicit recursion is a non-optimized bound where only existence of a fixed exponent is used.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result classifies faithful actions on manifolds. It gives no routine that a program could run, so there is nothing to implement beyond using the Lie-group conclusion as a mathematical guarantee. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The simulation reading is only qualitative: a continuous symmetry that is faithful on a boundaryless manifold is not a non-Lie group. The paper simulates nothing and gives no numerical scheme. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The argument is a contradiction proof built on sheaf categories and Witt groups. No checker or utility can be derived from the conclusion, since it asserts only that a kernel is nonzero. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The bound $L_d$ is shown to exist and may be taken as a power of $2$. This is an existence statement from exponent bookkeeping, not a procedure with defined inputs, outputs and cost, so no algorithm is extracted. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The test categories are abstract triangulated categories closed under direct summands. They have no finite representation to index or query, so they cannot serve as a data structure. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The only concrete computation is the point Witt group, an integer given by signature in degree zero. It is a classification, not a kernel suited to an accelerator, so no hardware mapping exists. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The group $\mathbb Z_p$ appears only as a topological profinite group acting on manifolds. The paper has no hardness assumption, adversary or security parameter, so no cryptographic claim follows. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The sphere Witt group computation yields one-dimensional rational spaces, which is pure algebraic topology. No loss, data distribution or learning guarantee is present. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-no-boundary** (hypothesis): The main theorems are stated only for manifolds without boundary; nothing is asserted when the manifold has a boundary. Blocked use: ['Applying the Lie-group conclusion to a group acting on a manifold with boundary']
- **limit-faithful-action** (hypothesis): The Lie-group conclusion needs a faithful action. The p-adic theorem only gives a nonzero kernel and a finite quotient, so non-faithful actions are not excluded. Blocked use: ['Concluding that a non-faithful action of a non-Lie group cannot occur', 'Reading the exclusion theorem as a statement about the unquotiented action']
- **limit-finite-dimension** (hypothesis): The dimension is a fixed finite integer at least one, and the odd stabilization dimension is chosen above it. The arguments give no statement in infinite dimension. Blocked use: ['Applying the theorem to infinite-dimensional manifolds or function spaces']
- **limit-odd-sphere** (hypothesis): The sphere lattice, degree action and contractible quotient results are stated for odd d at least three; even spheres are not covered by these statements. Blocked use: ['Using the lattice denominator for even-dimensional spheres']
- **limit-exponent-existence** (approximation): The exponent bookkeeping is explicitly not optimized and only the existence of a fixed exponent is used, so the recursion is an upper bound and not a sharp value. Blocked use: ['Presenting the recursion as the true torsion exponent', 'Using it as an optimality certificate']
- **limit-dense-doubling-hypotheses** (hypothesis): The doubling theorem needs a full replete duality-stable triangulated subcategory that is dense, and categories admitting triangular Witt groups; the exponent-two bound fails to follow without them. Blocked use: ['Applying the exponent-two bound to non-dense or non-duality-stable subcategories']
- **limit-nonconstructive-proof** (nonconstructive): The final step is a proof by contradiction from a supposed faithful action; no object or procedure is produced, and the odd-sphere step gives only existence of a degree. Blocked use: ['Extracting an algorithm or running time from the proof', 'Treating it as a decision procedure for faithfulness']
- **limit-restriction-caution** (formal_scope): Restriction does not preserve the test categories, and Verdier reflexivity is not stalkwise reflexivity; restrictions are taken in the ambient derived category. Blocked use: ['Assuming restrictions of test objects remain in the test categories', 'Assuming stalks are finite dimensional']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the Hilbert–Smith conjecture in every finite dimension: every locally compact second-countable Hausdorff group acting faithfully and jointly continuously on a connected Hausdorff second-countable finite-dimensional topological manifold without boundary is a Lie group.

<div class="center">

</div>

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

The Hilbert–Smith conjecture asks whether a locally compact group acting faithfully and continuously on a connected topological manifold must be a Lie group. An action is *faithful* when its kernel is trivial: no nonidentity element fixes every point. Faithfulness does not require point stabilizers to be trivial. We prove the following statement.

<a id="stmt-cor-lie"></a>
<div id="cor:lie" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (The Hilbert–Smith conjecture). *Let $n\geq1$. A locally compact second-countable Hausdorff group acting faithfully and jointly continuously on a connected Hausdorff second-countable topological $n$-manifold without boundary is a Lie group.*

</div>

This gives a positive resolution in every positive finite dimension. The manifold may be noncompact, nonorientable, or nontriangulable; fixed points and arbitrary stabilizers are allowed. In dimension zero, a connected manifold is a point, so a faithful acting group is trivial and the conclusion holds as well.

The mechanism is the exclusion of faithful actions of the additive group of $p$-adic integers, for a prime $p$, $$\mathbb Z_p=\varprojlim_k\mathbb Z/p^k\mathbb Z,$$ with its profinite topology. The precise classical reduction from the full group theorem to this case is recalled at the end of the proof.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2** ($p$-adic exclusion). *Let $n\geq1$ be an integer and $p$ a prime. Every jointly continuous action of $\mathbb Z_p$ on a connected Hausdorff second-countable topological $n$-manifold without boundary has nonzero kernel. Consequently the action factors through a finite quotient of $\mathbb Z_p$.*

</div>

For a manifold $M$, equip $\mathop{\mathrm{Homeo}}(M)$ with the compact-open topology. Jointly continuous actions are equivalently continuous homomorphisms $\mathbb Z_p\to\mathop{\mathrm{Homeo}}(M)$. No dimension bound on the orbit space is assumed.

also gives the dynamical consequence recorded in (Pardon 2013, Conjecture 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Pardon2013](#ref-Pardon2013)]. For a manifold $M$ as in [cor:lie](#cor:lie), a homeomorphism $f$ is *almost periodic* if the cyclic subgroup $\{f^j:j\in\mathbb Z\}$ has compact closure $K$ in $\mathop{\mathrm{Homeo}}(M)$. Some positive iterate of $f$ is then the time-one map of a continuous real flow: there are an integer $m\geq1$ and a continuous homomorphism $\Phi:\mathbb R\to\mathop{\mathrm{Homeo}}(M)$ with $\Phi(1)=f^m$. Indeed, $K$ is a compact abelian group acting faithfully and jointly continuously on $M$. Evaluation on a countable dense subset of $M$ embeds $K$ into a countable product of copies of $M$, so $K$ is second countable. By [cor:lie](#cor:lie), it is a compact abelian Lie group. Its identity component $K^0$ is a torus of finite index, so $f^m\in K^0$ for some $m\geq1$. The surjective exponential map of this torus places $f^m$ on a one-parameter subgroup, giving $\Phi$.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The problem and its predecessors

Hilbert’s fifth problem asked how far differentiability assumptions could be removed from the theory of continuous transformation groups (Hilbert 1902, Problem 5) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Hilbert1902](#ref-Hilbert1902)]. One central formulation concerns a group that is itself locally Euclidean. Gleason and Montgomery–Zippin resolved that formulation in 1952 (Gleason 1952; Montgomery and Zippin 1952) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Gleason1952](#ref-Gleason1952), [MontgomeryZippin1952](#ref-MontgomeryZippin1952)], and Yamabe extended the structure theory to general locally compact groups (Yamabe 1953b, 1953a) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Yamabe1953a](#ref-Yamabe1953a), [Yamabe1953b](#ref-Yamabe1953b)]. Hilbert–Smith asks for the restrictions imposed by a faithful action on a connected manifold. The passage to additive $p$-adic groups combines this structure theory with rigidity of periodic transformations. Lee gives a treatment of the reduction (Lee 1997) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Lee1997](#ref-Lee1997)]; Pardon’s introduction states the action-specific form used here (Pardon 2013, Introduction, p. 1) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Pardon2013](#ref-Pardon2013)]. The structure theory is developed systematically in Montgomery–Zippin (Montgomery and Zippin 1955) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[MontgomeryZippin1955](#ref-MontgomeryZippin1955)].

The low-dimensional cases illustrate how much geometry may be needed to control a merely continuous action. The cases of one- and two-manifolds were classical; Pardon gives a later account and proof of the two-dimensional case, crediting Montgomery–Zippin (Pardon 2019) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Pardon2019](#ref-Pardon2019)]. Pardon proved the three-dimensional conjecture (Pardon 2013, Theorem 1.5) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Pardon2013](#ref-Pardon2013)]. His local argument uses incompressible surfaces in a suitable invariant open set and the resulting action on a surface mapping class group. In particular, it accommodates arbitrary stabilizers without replacing the given action by a smooth action.

Another line of work controls the action by its regularity. Bochner–Montgomery established the differentiable precedent (Bochner and Montgomery 1946) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[BochnerMontgomery1946](#ref-BochnerMontgomery1946)]. Repovš–Ščepin proved the Lipschitz case on Riemannian manifolds (Repovš and Ščepin 1997, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[RepovsScepin1997](#ref-RepovsScepin1997)], and Maleshich obtained an obstruction for Hölder actions on closed manifolds when a common exponent exceeds $n/(n+2)$ (Maleshich 1997, Theorem 3.1) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Maleshich1997](#ref-Maleshich1997)]. Martin established the quasiconformal case on Riemannian manifolds (Martin 1999, Theorems 1.2–1.3) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Martin1999](#ref-Martin1999)]. These results impose regularity on the transformations, rather than only on the underlying manifold.

The metric arguments connect to the dimension-raising theory of Yang and Bredon–Raymond–Williams (Yang 1960; Bredon, Raymond, and Williams 1961) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Yang1960](#ref-Yang1960), [BredonRaymondWilliams1961](#ref-BredonRaymondWilliams1961)]. Their obstruction concerns integral cohomological dimension; Raymond’s subsequent analysis emphasizes the different behavior with field coefficients (Raymond 1961) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Raymond1961](#ref-Raymond1961)]. In the Lipschitz proof, an averaged invariant metric keeps Hausdorff dimension small enough to contradict the required dimension increase of the orbit space. Mj extended this approach to uniformly quasisymmetric actions on compact metric cohomology manifolds under Ahlfors regularity and Hausdorff-dimension hypotheses, with applications to boundaries of Poincaré duality hyperbolic groups (Mj 2012, Theorem 2.24 and Corollary 2.25) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Mj2012](#ref-Mj2012)].

Shelukhin used Floer-theoretic methods to exclude nontrivial continuous $p$-adic actions by homeomorphisms in the $C^0$-closure of the Hamiltonian diffeomorphism group of a closed symplectic manifold on whose $\pi_2$ both the symplectic class and the first Chern class vanish (Shelukhin 2024, Theorem A) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Shelukhin2024](#ref-Shelukhin2024)].

Newman’s rigidity theorem for periodic transformations (Newman 1931) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Newman1931](#ref-Newman1931)] supplies the local input used here: a finite-order homeomorphism of a connected manifold that fixes a nonempty open set pointwise is the identity. For a modern statement and proof, see (Pardon 2019, author manuscript, pp. 2–3) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Pardon2019](#ref-Pardon2019)]. Pardon’s local reduction (Pardon 2013, sec. 4.1) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Pardon2013](#ref-Pardon2013)] uses this rigidity to find a faithful action of an open subgroup on an invariant open subset of one coordinate chart. We use that reduction without imposing regularity on the transformations or on the boundary of the chart subset.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Duality and signatures

The signature viewpoint has a separate line of antecedents. Verdier developed duality for sheaves on locally compact spaces (Verdier 1966) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Verdier1966](#ref-Verdier1966)]; we use its derived functor formulation in Kashiwara–Schapira and Schapira (Kashiwara and Schapira 1990; Schapira 2023) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[KashiwaraSchapira1990](#ref-KashiwaraSchapira1990), [SchapiraNotes](#ref-SchapiraNotes)]. Balmer’s triangular Witt groups package symmetric forms and their neutral relations in categories with duality (Balmer 2000) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Balmer2000](#ref-Balmer2000)]. For localization we use Balmer–Walter’s published Theorem 2.1, which removes the weak-cancellation hypothesis of the earlier localization theorem (Balmer and Walter 2002) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[BalmerWalter2002](#ref-BalmerWalter2002)]. Woolf applied this formalism both to perfect complexes of sheaves and to constructible complexes. Using duality on perfect complexes, he proved a cylinder homotopy relation for that theory and obtained a generalized homology theory from constructible Witt groups on compact polyhedra (Woolf 2008) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[Woolf2008](#ref-Woolf2008)]. Ranicki–Weiss construct rational Pontryagin classes using a local chain theory generated by continuous simplex maps, with cutoff by subdivision, excision, and control of passage to direct summands (Ranicki and Weiss 2010, sec. 4 and Theorems 8.2–8.3) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[RanickiWeiss2010](#ref-RanickiWeiss2010)]. Their chain-level $L$-theory differs from the sheaf Witt groups used here, but its generated-category strategy is a close antecedent.

Our categories are generated by proper images of finite polyhedra under arbitrary continuous maps. Such images need not be constructible. We verify that duality and the constructions in the cylinder relation restrict to these specified generated categories. The additional quotient comparison and uniform integral bound establish the fixed signature lattice. The comparison is integral before rationalization: a rational isomorphism alone would not bound the possible denominators.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### The obstruction: equal parts in a fixed lattice

The numerical contradiction is simple. Fix an odd integer $d\geq3$; for an action on an $n$-manifold, we will take $d>n$. We construct an abelian group $E_d(S^d)$, an orientation class $u_d$, and a positive integer $L_d$. For an integral class $x$, write $\overline{x}=x\otimes1$ for its rational image. Then <a id="intro:lattice"></a>$$\label{intro:lattice}
 E_d(S^d)\otimes\mathbb Q=\mathbb Q\overline{u}_d,
 \qquad
 \mathop{\mathrm{im}}\bigl(E_d(S^d)\to E_d(S^d)\otimes\mathbb Q\bigr)
       \subseteq L_d^{-1}\mathbb Z\overline{u}_d.$$ A faithful $\mathbb Z_p$-action will produce, for every $k\geq1$, $p^k$ integral classes in this group. Their rational images are equal and sum to $4\overline{u}_d$. Each image is therefore $(4/p^k)\overline{u}_d$, which contradicts [intro:lattice](#intro:lattice) as soon as $p^k>4L_d$. The order of these choices matters: $L_d$ is fixed before $k$.

Here “integral” refers to an element of the abelian Witt group before it is tensored with $\mathbb Q$; the sheaf coefficients are real throughout. For a map from a compact polyhedron, its direct-image sheaf complex records the cohomology of the fibers. A symmetric self-duality on that complex supplies signature data, and the Witt group sets forms with a lagrangian equal to zero. This makes the invariant additive while retaining the integrality of ordinary signature.

Here is the group underlying this argument. For a finite polyhedron $X$, let $\mathcal T(X)$ be the full thick subcategory of the bounded derived category of real sheaves generated by $$Rf_*\underline{\mathbb R}_{B},\qquad f:B\longrightarrow X,
 \qquad B\text{ a compact finite polyhedron},$$ where $f$ is continuous. Thus an object is obtained by finite sums, shifts, cones, and direct summands from these proper direct images. Their stalks need not be finite dimensional. Verdier duality nevertheless restricts to this category, as we prove from simplex generators. We put $E_r(X)=W_r(\mathcal T(X))$, the Witt group of nonsingular symmetric forms for the shifted duality $\mathcal D_{X,r}=[-r]\mathcal D_X$, with neutral forms set equal to zero. [sec:categories](#sec:categories) gives the precise definitions, the signs, and homotopy invariance.

To establish [intro:lattice](#intro:lattice), we compare with the subcategory generated by semialgebraic maps. On a suitably chosen oriented ball, finite Witt relations can be evaluated by ordinary integral signature. Two arguments transfer that integrality to the continuous category. First, cutting off the finite polyhedral sources identifies quotient morphisms with germs near a closed set. Second, a full dense triangulated inclusion with duality, in which every object of the larger category is a summand of one from the smaller category, admits a map on Witt groups in the reverse direction whose two composites are multiplication by $2$. The resulting exponent-two kernel and cokernel bound is a consequence of Hornbostel–Schlichting’s cofinality theorem (Hornbostel and Schlichting 2004, author preprint, Appendix A, Theorem A.2) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[HornbostelSchlichting2004](#ref-HornbostelSchlichting2004)]. We give the cutoff and explicit doubling constructions in [sec:localization](#sec:localization); neither requires the images of arbitrary continuous maps to be constructible.

Localization on a finite cover of the sphere then bounds both the kernel and the cokernel of comparison by a fixed power of $2$, in every degree. This proves the lattice statement in [thm:sphere-lattice](#thm:sphere-lattice). Rational equivalence alone would lose the denominator bound. The cutoff description and the integral bound for dense inclusions are also useful independently of the group action; they supply the additional comparison needed beyond the constructible homology theory of (Woolf 2008) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[Woolf2008](#ref-Woolf2008)].

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### How the action supplies the equal parts

Suppose that a faithful action exists on an $n$-manifold. By Newman’s theorem, a small open subgroup $G\cong\mathbb Z_p$ acts faithfully and preserves orientation on a connected invariant open subset $O\subset\mathbb R^n$ of one coordinate chart. Choose an odd integer $d>n$, with $d\geq3$, and give $$W=O\times\mathbb R^{d-n}\subset\mathbb R^d$$ the product action, trivial in the added coordinates. We use the one-point compactification $W^+$, its orbit space $Y=W^+/G$, and the maps $$S^d\xrightarrow{c}W^+\xrightarrow{q}Y\xrightarrow{t}X.$$ Here $c$ collapses $S^d\setminus W$ to $\infty$, and $t$ is a continuous map into a compact space $X$ homeomorphic to a finite polyhedron. We require the value $b=tq(\infty)$ to have a basis of contractible open neighborhoods in $X$. The composite $tqc$ always has the same finite-polyhedron source $S^d$, as shown in [fig:local-test](#fig:local-test).

<figure id="fig:local-test">

<figcaption>A local action produces compact-source tests. Away from $b = tq(∞)$, the source of $f$ lies in the oriented manifold $W = O × ℝ^{d − n}$. The restricted map $f^{−1}(X\{b}) → X \ {b}$ is proper, with oriented manifold source.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-local-test"></a>
**Figure source and visual witness** (`fig:local-test`)

Put $f_t=tqc$ and $A_t=Rf_{t*}\underline{\mathbb C}_{S^d}$. Here $\mathbb C$ is the positive real plane with pairing $B(z,w)=\operatorname{Re}(z\overline w)$. Pushing this coefficient pairing together with the orientation of $S^d$ gives the degree-$d$ form $\alpha_t$ on $A_t$. Away from $b$, the map $f_t$ is proper from an oriented open subset of $W$. Identify the characters of $G$ with the group $\Lambda=\bigcup_{j\geq0}\mu_{p^j}$ of roots of unity of $p$-power order. A finite partition $\Lambda=D_0\sqcup\cdots\sqcup D_{m-1}$ gives orthogonal projectors $e_i$ on $A_t|_{X\setminus\{b\}}$ onto the corresponding character parts. A projector need not extend to a summand over $b$. Choose a contractible open neighborhood $U$ of $b$. The projector’s germ near $X\setminus U$ defines an endomorphism of $A_t$ in the quotient by objects compactly supported in $U$.

For a self-adjoint projector $e_i$, the operator $2e_i-1$ is a self-adjoint involution. We therefore have the following sum of forms on the existing object: $$[A_t,\alpha_t]+[A_t,\alpha_t(2e_i-1)].$$ An integral localization isomorphism lifts it to a class $z_i(t)\in E_d(X)$. An explicit matrix isometry gives $\sum_i z_i(t)=2[A_t,\alpha_t]$, without taking the images of quotient idempotents.

Averaging a map supported in a ball gives a test $a:Y\to S^d$ with $\deg(aqc)=1$. For each $k$, faithfulness gives a nonempty open region $V\subset W/G$ over which $W/(p^kG)\to W/G$ consists of $p^k$ labeled sheets. Serre’s results on odd spheres provide a map $D_s:S^d\to S^d$ of degree $s>0$ such that $D_s a$ is homotopic to a map constant outside $V$ (Serre 1951, 1953) <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[Serre1951](#ref-Serre1951), [Serre1953](#ref-Serre1953)]. There a locally constant coefficient isometry permutes $p^k$ chosen character parts cyclically. Covariance and homotopy invariance equate the rational images $s\overline{z}_i(a)$. Cancelling the nonzero rational scalar $s$ equates the $\overline{z}_i(a)$, while the original integral classes stay in the same fixed lattice. Their rational images sum to $4\overline{u}_d$: one factor of $2$ comes from the real coefficient plane and the other from the involution construction. The fixed lattice gives the contradiction.

Only the actual manifold sources and the test spaces carry Verdier duality in this argument. The compact orbit spaces enter through ordinary sheaf direct images, averaging, and Čech cohomology. No dimension bound on the orbit spaces or regularity assumption on the boundary of $O$ is used. The odd stabilization is the only dimensional choice. In particular, the proof treats both odd congruence classes of $d$ modulo $4$.

construct and bound the signature invariant. [sec:local-tests](#sec:local-tests) prepares the local tests, including the odd-sphere argument. [sec:characters](#sec:characters) constructs the integral character classes and proves [thm:main](#thm:main), [cor:lie](#cor:lie).

<a id="sec:categories"></a>
### Compact-source sheaf categories and homotopy invariance

The sheaves used below can have very complicated stalks. Their finiteness comes from their sources: they are built, by finitely many operations, from proper images of constant sheaves on finite polyhedra. We first establish duality on exactly these categories, and then prove the cylinder relation that will make their Witt groups homotopy invariant.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Spaces, coefficients, and generators

All sheaves in this section have coefficients in the field $\mathbb R$, regarded algebraically. A *test space* is a locally compact, finite-dimensional, separable metrizable space of the following kinds: a finite polyhedron, a space homeomorphic to one, an open subset of such a space, or a finite product of these spaces. Points and the empty space are included. The spaces to which Verdier duality is applied will always be test spaces.

Write $$\mathsf D(X)=D^b(\operatorname{Sh}_{\mathbb R}(X)).$$ For a complex $A$, the assertion that its support is contained in a compact set $K\subset X$ means that all its cohomology sheaves vanish on $X\setminus
K$. We use this formulation even when the set of points with a nonzero cohomology stalk is not closed. All tensor products are derived tensor products over $\mathbb R$; tensoring sheaves is exact over this field.

<a id="stmt-def-test-categories"></a>
<div id="def:test-categories" class="definition">

<a id="cat:generators"></a>
**Definition 3** (The test categories). Let $\mathcal T^0(X)$ be the smallest full replete triangulated subcategory of $\mathsf D(X)$ containing $$\label{cat:generators}
 Rf_*\underline{\mathbb R}_{B},
 \qquad f:B\longrightarrow X,
 \qquad B\text{ a compact finite polyhedron},$$ where $f$ is any continuous map. Let $\mathcal T(X)$ be its closure under direct summands in $\mathsf D(X)$.

When $X$ has a specified semialgebraic model, define $\mathcal P^0(X)$ and $\mathcal P(X)$ in the same manner, requiring $B$ to be a compact polyhedron in a Euclidean space and $f$ to be continuous semialgebraic in the specified models. For an arbitrary open subset $U$ of a semialgebraic model, the condition on $f:B\to U$ is that its composite into that model be semialgebraic. The open subset itself need not be semialgebraic.

An allowed map for $\mathcal T$ is a continuous map of test spaces. For $\mathcal P$ we use continuous semialgebraic maps in the models, their restrictions to the open subsets under consideration, and inclusions of such open subsets. The condition on a map used in the arguments below is that composition carry every allowed compact-source generator to an allowed compact-source generator. Homotopies for $\mathcal P$ satisfy this condition in their two variables; the continuous semialgebraic homotopies used below do so.

</div>

The sheaf operations used below satisfy their finite-dimensionality hypotheses for every allowed map, including a nonproper map. To see this explicitly, every test space is homeomorphic to a locally closed subset of some $\mathbb R^N$: realize its finite polyhedral factors as closed Euclidean subsets, take the prescribed relative open subsets, and then their finite product. Fix such an embedding of the source $X$ of a continuous map $g:X\to Y$. Since $Y$ is Hausdorff, each fiber $Z=g^{-1}(y)$ is closed in $X$, hence locally closed in the same $\mathbb R^N$. For its embedding $i:Z\hookrightarrow\mathbb R^N$, extension $i_!$ is exact: factor $i$ as a closed inclusion followed by an open inclusion. For every sheaf $F$ on $Z$, composition of derived proper images gives $$R\Gamma_c(Z;F)\simeq R\Gamma_c(\mathbb R^N;i_!F).$$ The right side has no cohomology above $N$, by (Schapira 2023, Lemma 11.7.1) <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[SchapiraNotes](#ref-SchapiraNotes)]. This bound is independent of $y$. Thus every such $g$ has finite $c$-soft dimension, by (Schapira 2023, Definition 11.3.1 and Remark 11.3.3) <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[SchapiraNotes](#ref-SchapiraNotes)]. The composition formula used here is established before that finite-dimension hypothesis is imposed (Schapira 2023, Equation (11.3.1)) <a id="pm-cite-32"></a>[[SchapiraNotes](#ref-SchapiraNotes)], so this verification does not assume the duality formalism it is used to justify.

In particular the maps in <a href="#cat:generators" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cat:generators">[cat:generators]</a> are proper, because their sources are compact and their targets are Hausdorff, and their derived images are bounded. Proper base change identifies their stalk cohomology with the cohomology of compact fibers. Boundedness does not imply that these stalks are finite dimensional. For the general locally compact sheaf formalism see (Kashiwara and Schapira 1990, vol. 292, chap. III) <a id="pm-cite-33"></a>[[KashiwaraSchapira1990](#ref-KashiwaraSchapira1990)] and (Schapira 2023, secs. 11.3–11.4).

We work with essentially small categories. This causes no size issue: there is a set of finite polyhedra up to isomorphism and of continuous maps from each into a fixed $X$; the derived category is locally small. Finite constructions using their morphisms, followed by the choice of summands, still have a set of isomorphism classes. Choose representatives and then take replete subcategories when convenient.

<a id="stmt-cat-simplex-generators"></a>
<div id="cat:simplex-generators" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4** (Simplex generators and summands). *In either definition, closed simplices suffice in place of all compact finite polyhedra. Every object of $\mathcal T(X)$, respectively $\mathcal P(X)$, is a direct summand of an object of $\mathcal T^0(X)$, respectively $\mathcal P^0(X)$. In particular these summand closures are triangulated and thick.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cat-simplex-generators"><summary>Proof of stmt-cat-simplex-generators</summary>

<a id="cat:simplex-boundary-triangle"></a>
*Proof.* Fix a finite triangulation of $B$. For a closed simplex $\sigma$, let $j_\sigma:\sigma^\circ\hookrightarrow\sigma$ and $i_\sigma:\partial\sigma\hookrightarrow\sigma$ be the inclusions. The open-complement triangle is $$\label{cat:simplex-boundary-triangle}
 (j_\sigma)_!\underline{\mathbb R}_{\sigma^\circ}
 \longrightarrow\underline{\mathbb R}_{\sigma}
 \longrightarrow(i_\sigma)_*\underline{\mathbb R}_{\partial\sigma}
 \longrightarrow
 (j_\sigma)_!\underline{\mathbb R}_{\sigma^\circ}[1].$$ Induction on dimension expresses the first term using constants on closed simplices: apply the same argument to the finite triangulation of the boundary, starting with points. Filter $B$ by its skeleta. On each skeleton the complement of the previous skeleton is a finite disjoint union of open simplices. Its constant sheaf extended by zero is the finite sum of the first terms of <a href="#cat:simplex-boundary-triangle" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cat:simplex-boundary-triangle">[cat:simplex-boundary-triangle]</a>, extended into that skeleton. The open-complement triangles for this filtration therefore build $\underline{\mathbb R}_{B}$ from constants on the closed simplices of $B$. Pushing these finite triangles along $f$ proves the assertion. For the semialgebraic categories all restrictions and simplex inclusions just used are semialgebraic.

For the second assertion, the class of summands of objects in a full triangulated subcategory is itself triangulated. Indeed, suppose $A\oplus A'=C$ and $B\oplus B'=D$ belong to that subcategory, and let $u:A\to B$. Fullness places the block map $u\oplus0:C\to D$ in the subcategory. A cone on this map is $$\mathop{\mathrm{Cone}}(u)\oplus B'\oplus A'[1].$$ Hence $\mathop{\mathrm{Cone}}(u)$ is a summand of an object of the subcategory. Shifts and finite sums have the same property, and an iterated summand is a summand. This proves the claim and also proves thickness. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Duality on the generated categories

Let $a_X:X\to\mathrm{pt}$. We use the dualizing complex and Verdier operation $$\omega_X=a_X^!\mathbb R,
 \qquad
 \mathcal D_X A=R\mathcal Hom(A,\omega_X).$$ The operation is initially understood in the ambient derived category. Its boundedness and the invertibility of its biduality map on our categories will be proved, not assumed.

<a id="cat:verdier-formula"></a>
We recall precisely the pieces of the sheaf formalism used here. For locally compact spaces and maps of finite $c$-soft dimension, $Rf_!$ has right adjoint $f^!$, composition identifies $f^!\omega_Y$ with $\omega_X$, and the counit gives the trace <a id="cat:trace"></a>$$\label{cat:trace}
 \operatorname{Tr}_f:Rf_!\omega_X\longrightarrow\omega_Y.$$ The internal duality isomorphism is $$\label{cat:verdier-formula}
 Rf_*\mathcal D_X A\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal D_Y Rf_!A
 \qquad(A\in\mathsf D(X)).$$ It is adjoint to evaluation followed by the trace, and is compatible with the evaluation map $\eta_A:A\to\mathcal D_X^2A$ whenever the expressions are bounded. For an open inclusion $j$, $j^!=j^{-1}$, so duality commutes with open restriction. These are the adjunction and internal duality statements in (Schapira 2023, Theorem 11.4.2, Propositions 11.4.3–11.4.4 and 11.4.6(b)) <a id="pm-cite-34"></a>[[SchapiraNotes](#ref-SchapiraNotes)]. Proper base change, the projection formula, and compact-support Künneth will also be used in their natural forms (Schapira 2023, Theorems 11.3.5–11.3.6 and Corollary 11.3.11) <a id="pm-cite-35"></a>[[SchapiraNotes](#ref-SchapiraNotes)]. None of these statements asserts reflexivity of every bounded sheaf complex.

<a id="stmt-prop-test-duality"></a>
<div id="prop:test-duality" class="proposition">

**Proposition 5** (Duality and compact support). *The Verdier operation restricts to a triangulated duality on each of $\mathcal T^0(X)$, $\mathcal T(X)$, $\mathcal P^0(X)$, and $\mathcal P(X)$. In particular, for every object $A$ in one of these categories, $\mathcal D_X A$ belongs to the same category and the canonical map $$\eta_A:A\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal D_X^2A$$ is an isomorphism. There is a compact $K\subset X$ outside which both $A$ and $\mathcal D_X A$ vanish.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-test-duality"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-test-duality</summary>

<a id="cat:simplex-duality"></a>
*Proof.* We first calculate on a closed $n$-simplex $\Delta$, choosing an orientation of its interior. With $j:\Delta^\circ\hookrightarrow
\Delta$, $$\label{cat:simplex-duality}
 \mathcal D_\Delta\underline{\mathbb R}_{\Delta}
   =\omega_\Delta
   \simeq j_!\underline{\mathbb R}_{\Delta^\circ}[n],
 \qquad
 \mathcal D_\Delta\bigl(j_!\underline{\mathbb R}_{\Delta^\circ}[n]\bigr)
   \simeq\underline{\mathbb R}_{\Delta}.$$ Here is the local calculation, including the boundary. At an interior point the compactly supported cohomology of a sufficiently small open ball is $\mathbb R$ in degree $n$ and zero otherwise. At a boundary point a cofinal system of relative neighborhoods is homeomorphic to a half-ball with its flat face retained. All its compactly supported cohomology vanishes. For example, its one-point compactification is a closed ball and has zero reduced cohomology; this also applies at corners after a local homeomorphism. The local description of the dualizing complex therefore gives the first isomorphism in <a href="#cat:simplex-duality" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cat:simplex-duality">[cat:simplex-duality]</a>. The orientation identifies its interior local system with the constant sheaf.
